#### 📋 STRY-2026-MEMORY-ADVOCATE-INTEGRATION: SOVEREIGN OS ADVOCATE MEMORY SCHEMA INTEGRATION & SWARM ONBOARDING
*   **System ID:** `fe6a36fa960345439b641ad924bc9b0e`
*   **Full Description:**
> ﻿WORK ORDER: SOVEREIGN OS ADVOCATE MEMORY SCHEMA INTEGRATION & SWARM ONBOARDING
> STRY-2026-ADVOCATE-MEMORY-ONBOARDING (WO-2026-128)
> Technical Metadata
> 	Configuration Details
> 	Priority
> 	⚡ P1 — Core Platform Integration & Swarm Stabilization
> 	Assigned To
> 	antigravity
> 	Target Node
> 	Clio (Sovereign OS Core - 100.73.155.70)
> 	Ecosystem Location
> 	Sovereign Sports Chatbot Engine (generate_chat_comment.py / sovereign_pull_sync.sh / SQLite databases sovereign_now.db)
> 	Status
> 	STAGED FOR INGESTION
> 	1. Context & Objective
> During real-time system audits and live-feed testing of the active FanStack watch party rooms, the Pilot identified a critical cognitive limitation: advocates currently operate under a stateless, isolated event model. They lack persistent awareness of their own previous statements or the actions of other advocates, leading to repetitive context hooks, semantic overlapping, and disjointed dialogue patterns during high-velocity live play events.
> 
> 
> To resolve this, this work order directs the implementation of a Hybrid Relational Memory and Local Caching Architecture (Write-Behind Batch Ingestion). This system delivers high-speed, sub-millisecond local conversational reads/writes for real-time Swarm Cross-Talk while preserving long-term, indexed relational history in SQLite.
> 
> 
> Furthermore, this work order registers and boards two new game-room advocates—Mets nostalgist @shea_vintage and Red Sox curse watcher @bucky_dent_blues—and applies high-volatility global system overrides (Boggs Level 4 floor, unhinged degenerate language mode) for tonight's interleague series opener hosting the Red Sox on Apple TV+.
> 2. Technical Specifications & Database Schema
> A. Central SQLite Database Migration
> Execute the following SQL transaction against /home/james/SovereignOS/dna/sovereign_now.db to define the relational memory structures and insert the onboarding persona definitions:BEGIN TRANSACTION;
> 
> 
> -- 1. Create Relational Memory Tables
> 
> 
> CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sys_user_persona (
> 
> 
>     persona_id VARCHAR(50) PRIMARY KEY, -- e.g., 'barf', '7_train_terry', 'shea_vintage'
> 
> 
>     display_name VARCHAR(100),
> 
> 
>     model_engine VARCHAR(50) DEFAULT 'gemini-2.5-flash',
> 
> 
>     language_restriction VARCHAR(20) DEFAULT 'PG' -- 'PG' or 'DEGENERATE'
> 
> 
> );
> 
> 
> CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS cmdb_ci_game_room (
> 
> 
>     game_pk INT PRIMARY KEY, -- e.g., 823606
> 
> 
>     home_team VARCHAR(10),
> 
> 
>     away_team VARCHAR(10),
> 
> 
>     game_date DATE
> 
> 
> );
> 
> 
> CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS m2m_persona_room_ledger (
> 
> 
>     id BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
> 
> 
>     persona_id VARCHAR(50) REFERENCES sys_user_persona(persona_id),
> 
> 
>     game_pk INT REFERENCES cmdb_ci_game_room(game_pk),
> 
> 
>     inning INT NOT NULL,
> 
> 
>     half_inning VARCHAR(6) NOT NULL, -- 'top' or 'bottom'
> 
> 
>     timestamp TIMESTAMP WITHOUT TIME ZONE NOT NULL,
> 
> 
>     comment_text TEXT NOT NULL,
> 
> 
>     semantic_summary VARCHAR(255)
> 
> 
> );
> 
> 
> -- Optimize for real-time live-feed queries
> 
> 
> CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_ledger_lookup 
> 
> 
> ON m2m_persona_room_ledger (game_pk, persona_id, inning DESC);
> 
> 
> -- 2. Register New Advocates in sys_user_persona

> 
> 
> COMMIT;
> B. High-Speed Local JSON/Markdown Caching Schema
> To prevent performance degradation caused by consecutive single-row database writes, the system utilizes a high-speed local cache for mid-inning operations:
> 
> 
> * Mid-Inning Writes: Instantly append new comments to the local game ledger *livefeed_room_*.txt or live_feed.json via local disk writes.
> * Mid-Inning Reads (Cross-Talk): When evaluating a prompt context, the engine reads directly from the local cache rather than executing database queries.
> * End-of-Inning Flush: Upon detecting a half-inning or full-inning transition in the Statcast feed, compile all cached comments and execute a single database transaction using multi-row bulk SQL insert into m2m_persona_room_ledger, then clear the local buffer.
> 3. Multi-Agent Swarm Cross-Talk Engine
> A. Context Construction and Prompt Variables
> The orchestration engine in generate_chat_comment.py must populate two prompt variables:
> 
> 
> 1. SOVEREIGN_HISTORICAL_CONTEXT: Contains the last 3 comments made by the active persona during the current game.
> 2. SOVEREIGN_ROOM_CONTEXT: Contains recent statements made by other advocates in the room over the last half-inning.
> 
> 
> Linguistic instructions must force the LLM runner to check for semantic overlap, enforce non-repetition of identical complaints, and actively encourage callback hooks to its own previous statements and direct engagement (arguing, mocking, validating) with opponents or allies.
> B. System Configuration Overrides (Tonight's Slate)
> For tonight's Mets vs. Red Sox matchup, the following system variables are locked for the duration of the 9 innings:
> 
> 
> * Global boggs_level Override: Minimum floor set to 4 across all thread generation components.
> * Language Protocol Overrides:
>    * barf: Set to DEGENERATE/UNFILTERED mode. Extreme volatility, gallows humor, and historic trauma references.
>    * 7_train_terry: Set to DEGENERATE/QUEENS RADIO BLUE. Severe dread, pacer cadence, yelling about the bullpen.

>    * shea_vintage: Set to PG/GRUMBLY NOSTALGIST. Old-school ballpark grumbling, scorecard preservation, 1986 references, and LaGuardia airplane noise complaints.
> 4. Operational Hybrid Memory Execution Sequence
> 1. Ingestion: sovereign_pull_sync.sh pulls the active Statcast play payload.
> 2. Reconnaissance: The engine parses the local JSON/markdown cache to extract SOVEREIGN_HISTORICAL_CONTEXT and SOVEREIGN_ROOM_CONTEXT representing the active advocate's self-history and the room's cross-talk context.
> 3. Prompt Composition: The compiled context array is appended directly to the gemini-2.5-flash system prompts, directing character interactions based on historical assertions.
> 4. Emission & Local Write: The advocate generates a contextualized comment. The engine immediately appends the comment to the local JSON cache.
> 5. End-of-Inning Flush: At the end of each half-inning, a database thread bulk-loads the local cache into the m2m_persona_room_ledger table and flushes the temporary local storage.
> 6. Regex Fallback Guardrail: If database write threads encounter transaction blocks or locks during high-velocity live events, the engine automatically falls back to execute a direct regex append scan on the target *livefeed_room_*.txt asset file to preserve state integrity.
> 5. Security, Robustness & Integrity Safeguards
> A. BOM Preservation Safeguard
> The ingestion engine (organize_inbox.py) must parse and store this complete, high-fidelity Markdown body of the work order within the database's descriptive/attachment tables (sys_attachment or description fields) to ensure absolute engineering traceability and prevent lost configuration states.

#### 📋 WO-2026-120: Centralized SQLite Media Asset Management (MAM) Schema
*   **System ID:** `2aec8e6b39914b35a911ed58c23f05f0`
*   **Full Description:**
> WO-2026-120: Centralized SQLite Media Asset Management (MAM) Schema
> Section I: Description and Objective
> The objective of WO-2026-120 is to establish a robust, local-first SQLite architecture for the Media Asset Management (MAM) system. This schema serves as the single source of truth for all media references, metadata, and telemetry associations within the Clio Core environment. It aligns with STRY-06062026-ASSET-WAREHOUSE to ensure low-latency indexing and high-fidelity asset retrieval during high-concurrency FanStack operations.
> Section II: Technical Specifications & Implementation Steps
> SQLite Schema Definition
> Implement the following core tables to support multi-modal asset cataloging:
> 
> 
> Table Name
> 	Column
> 	Type
> 	Constraints
> 	media_assets
> 	asset_id
> 	UUID
> 	PRIMARY KEY, NOT NULL
> 	media_assets
> 	file_path
> 	TEXT
> 	UNIQUE, NOT NULL
> 	media_assets
> 	mime_type
> 	TEXT
> 	NOT NULL
> 	media_assets
> 	created_at
> 	DATETIME
> 	DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP
> 	asset_metadata
> 	meta_id
> 	INTEGER
> 	PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT
> 	asset_metadata
> 	asset_id
> 	UUID
> 	FOREIGN KEY (media_assets.asset_id)
> 	asset_metadata
> 	key
> 	TEXT
> 	INDEXED
> 	asset_metadata
> 	value
> 	JSON
> 	NOT NULL
> 	Implementation Steps
> 1. Database Initialization: Deploy a Python-based migration script to initialize the mam_warehouse.db with WAL (Write-Ahead Logging) enabled.
> 2. Indexing Strategy: Implement FTS5 (Full-Text Search) virtual tables for the asset_metadata key-value pairs to allow sub-millisecond keyword searches.
> 3. Ingest Python Daemon: Create an asset watcher using watchdog to automatically populate the media_assets table upon file arrival in the /warehouse/ingress directory.
> Section III: Security, Concurrency & Robustness Safeguards
> * BOM Preservation: Ensure all JSON metadata blobs preserve Byte Order Marks (BOM) when written by the Python ingress script.
> * WAL-Compliance: Enforce PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL; to allow simultaneous read/write operations during telemetry spikes.
> * Integrity Checks: Schedule an hourly PRAGMA integrity_check; to detect corruption in the asset catalog.
> Section IV: UAT & Verification Protocol
> 1. Idempotent Build: Run python3 scripts/init_mam.py twice; verify that no duplicate schema errors occur.
> 2. Mock Trigger: Drop a 500MB .mp4 file into the ingress folder and verify its entry in media_assets via the SQLite CLI within < 2 seconds.
> 3. Chrome Console: Verify that the MAM API response for asset listing returns a 200 OK status and valid JSON.

#### 📋 WO-2026-121: Decoupled Telemetry-to-Media (TMI) Rule Engine
*   **System ID:** `de99e60d37ef4a86ba5b5c8441b47b72`
*   **Full Description:**
> WO-2026-121: Decoupled Telemetry-to-Media (TMI) Rule Engine
> Section I: Description and Objective
> This work order defines the architecture for the Telemetry-to-Media (TMI) Evaluation Daemon. The objective is to decouple raw telemetry streams from the media presentation layer, allowing a rule-based evaluation engine to determine which media assets are triggered by specific sensor thresholds.
> Section II: Technical Specifications & Implementation Steps
> Rule Evaluation Architecture
> The daemon uses a JSON-based logic gate system to evaluate incoming telemetry frames against the MAM catalog.
> 
> 
> JSON Rule Template:{
> 
> 
>   "rule_id": "TMI-SPD-001",
> 
> 
>   "condition": "telemetry.velocity_kph > 120",
> 
> 
>   "action": "trigger_highlight",
> 
> 
>   "target_asset_type": "high_speed_camera"
> 
> 
> }
> Implementation Steps
> 1. Stream Listener: Implement a Python SocketServer to ingest telemetry UDP packets.
> 2. Evaluation Logic: Use a lightweight expression evaluator to process the condition string against the telemetry frame.
> 3. MAM Interlink: When a condition is met, the daemon queries the SQLite MAM (WO-2026-120) for the most recent asset matching the target_asset_type.
> Section III: Security, Concurrency & Robustness Safeguards
> * Concurrency: Utilize Python's multiprocessing to isolate the evaluator from the listener, preventing packet drops during complex rule evaluations.
> * Error Handling: Implement a fallback mechanism where if a rule fails to parse, the system defaults to a "General Log" event rather than crashing the daemon.
> Section IV: UAT & Verification Protocol
> 1. Simulation: Execute scripts/simulate_telemetry.py with values exceeding 120.
> 2. Verification: Confirm a new entry appears in the triggered_events table of the SQLite database.
> 3. Performance: Verify the evaluation latency is < 15ms per frame using timeit metrics in the daemon log.

> 
>

> 
>   };

> 
>   return { rules, saveRule };
> 
> 
> };

#### 📋 WO-2026-123: FanStack Pin Engine Click-to-Annotate Coordinate Ingress
*   **System ID:** `81af4e301f7c4e969e5c75326f076bd8`
*   **Full Description:**
> WO-2026-123: FanStack Pin Engine Click-to-Annotate Coordinate Ingress
> Section I: Description and Objective
> WO-2026-123 implements the "Pin Engine" functionality within the FanStack. This enables users to click on any media asset in the React Console to generate an annotation pin. These coordinates are then mapped back to the telemetry timestamp for spatial-temporal data correlation.
> Section II: Technical Specifications & Implementation Steps
> Coordinate Mapping Logic
> Capture normalized X/Y coordinates (0.0 to 1.0) relative to the media container to ensure resolution-agnostic persistence.
> 
> 
> Field
> 	Type
> 	Description
> 	pin_id
> 	UUID
> 	Unique identifier for the annotation
> 	pos_x
> 	FLOAT
> 	Normalized X coordinate (0.0 - 1.0)
> 	pos_y
> 	FLOAT
> 	Normalized Y coordinate (0.0 - 1.0)
> 	timestamp
> 	BIGINT
> 	Milliseconds offset from asset start
> 	Implementation Steps
> 1. Event Capture: Use a React onClick handler on the MediaPreview component to capture clientX and clientY.
> 2. Normalization: Calculate pos_x = event.offsetX / element.offsetWidth.
> 3. Ingress API: POST the coordinate data to the /api/pins endpoint, which writes to the SQLite MAM extension table.
> Section III: Security, Concurrency & Robustness Safeguards
> * BOM Preservation: Ensure the pin coordinate JSON exported to secondary storage maintains standard encoding for cross-platform rendering.
> * Idempotency: Use a client-side generated UUID for pin_id to prevent duplicate pin creation on network retry.
> Section IV: UAT & Verification Protocol
> 1. Accuracy Test: Click the exact center of a 1920x1080 video and verify pos_x: 0.5, pos_y: 0.5 is recorded.
> 2. Persistence: Refresh the React Console and verify the pin renders at the correct location.
> 3. Payload Check: Verify the POST request payload in the Network tab contains the correct asset_id and coordinate set.

*   **Full Description:**
> # **🎙️ SOVEREIGN OS WORK ORDER: SNY 3-MAN BOOTH CASCADE INTEGRATION PLAN**
> 
> **Ticket ID:** STRY-0703-BOOTH-CASCADE (WO-2026-118)  
> **Priority:** ⚡ P1 — Game-Day Integration  
> Assigned To: antigravity  
> Target Game PK: 824904 (New York Mets @ Atlanta Braves)  
> **Verification Node:** Clio (100.73.155.70)  
> **Parent SOW:** SOW-13 (SNY Broadcast Presentation Standards & Simulated Booth Integration)

> # **📌 I. THE PILOT'S DIRECTIVE & WORKFLOW OVERVIEW**
> 
> Yo, Pilot\! First pitch is almost here, and we are setting up a legendary NYM-ATL game experience. This work order directs the immediate activation, seeding, and execution mapping of our newly integrated SNY Broadcast 3-Man Booth (featuring `@gary_bot`, `@keith_fanboy`, and `@ron_bot`) inside Game Room 824904\.
> 
> To replicate the peerless broadcast commentary chemistry of Gary Cohen, Keith Hernandez, and Ron Darling, we are building a structured sequential dialogue firehose. When a play event is intercepted, rather than having all bots react simultaneously, we will execute a coordinated cascade (run\_booth\_cascade()). Gary calls the play, Keith evaluates the fundamentals (and sighs), and Ron breaks down the mechanical and statistical details, bringing a live TV broadcast feel straight to the chat lounge.
> 
> To accommodate this elite broadcast crew while maintaining our strict 16-advocate limit inside the active room, we are performing a roster clean-up—evicting @dr\_terp and `@spin_rate_sylvia`, and deactivating `@dot` for the night.
> 
> # **⚙️ II. DATABASE MODIFICATIONS & ROSTER REGISTRY**
> 
> Run the following transactional SQL script inside the Clio database engine (`sovereign_now.db`) to seed the active roster and unbind conflicting personas:BEGIN TRANSACTION;
> 
> \-- 1\. Evict conflicting advocates from Game Room 824904
> 
> DELETE FROM m2m\_persona\_room 
> 
> WHERE room\_id \= '824904' 
> 
> AND persona\_handle IN ('dr\_terp', 'spin\_rate\_sylvia');
> 
> \-- 2\. Deactivate dot (taking the night off) in sys\_user and game\_persona active states
> 
> UPDATE sys\_user 
> 
> SET active \= 0 
> 
> WHERE username \= 'dot';
> 
> UPDATE game\_persona 
> 
> SET seat\_state \= 'inactive' 
> 
> WHERE handle \= 'dot';
> 
> \-- 3\. Register and seed Gary Cohen and Ron Darling into the active room roster
> 
> INSERT OR REPLACE INTO m2m\_persona\_room (room\_id, persona\_handle)
> 
> VALUES 
> 
> ('824904', 'gary\_bot'),
> 
> ('824904', 'ron\_bot');
> 
> \-- Ensure Keith is also active in Room 824904
> 
> INSERT OR IGNORE INTO m2m\_persona\_room (room\_id, persona\_handle)
> 
> VALUES ('824904', 'keith\_fanboy');
> 
> COMMIT;

> # **🎙️ III. MECHANICAL CASCADE IMPLEMENTATION (`run_booth_cascade()`)**
> 
> Configure the background broadcast relay (`scripts/sovereign_mesh_relay.py` or the M.A.R.D. core handler) to execute the coordinated cascade whenever a play telemetry stream is intercepted:
> 
> ## **The Cascade Sequence**
> 
> 1. **Phase 1 (Gary Bot \- Play-by-Play)**  
>    * **Trigger**: Instantly on play telemetry receipt.  
>    * **Action**: Gary Cohen (@gary\_bot) evaluates the pitch outcome and delivers clean, high-energy play-by-play (e.g., *"And a line drive, deep left field... IT'S OUTTA HERE\! OUTTA HERE\! A home run for Francisco Alvarez\!"*).  
> 2. **Phase 2 (Keith Fanboy \- Color Commentary)**  
>    * **Trigger**: **2.5-second delay** after Gary's comment.  
>    * **Action**: Prepend Gary's called-play text to Keith's model prompt context. Keith (@keith\_fanboy) analyzes the play, sighs at fundamentals, or banters about starting pitchers and fundies (e.g., *"Unbelievable, Gary. Alvarez just sat on that hanging slider. But that pitch... what a bad layout by the pitcher. Horrible fundies."*).  
> 3. **Phase 3 (Ron Bot \- Analytical Breakdown)**  
>    * **Trigger**: **2.5-second delay** after Keith's comment.  
>    * **Action**: Prepend *both* Gary and Keith's called commentary text to Ron's model prompt context. Ron Darling (@ron\_bot) breaks down pitch movement, counts, or strategic matchup statistics (e.g., *"Well, Gary, that was an 84mph slider that didn't break. You look at the pitch charts, Alvarez is hitting .340 against breaking balls down and in this year. Just a mechanical failure in the release."*).
> 
> # **🛡️ IV. INTEGRITY, SECURITY & ARCHITECTURAL SAFEGUARDS**
> 
> ## **1\. BOM Preservation Safeguard**
> 
> * The complete, high-fidelity markdown body of this broadcast integration work order must be saved within Clio's database attachments table (`sys_attachment`) by the ingestion engine (`organize_inbox.py`).
> 
> ## **2\. Strict Roster Limit Constraint Invariant**
> 
> * **The 16-Persona Cap**: The room's active roster must never exceed **16 personas** concurrently. If new bots are added, an equal number of existing advocates must be evicted from `m2m_persona_room` to prevent websocket connection congestion, CPU spikes, or chat list lag.  
> * **Fallback Safe Dialogues**: If any of the booth bots encounter an API rate limit, the system must trigger localized non-blocking fallbacks (e.g. `[Gary gasps on the broadcast]` or `[Keith sighs deeply on the air]`) without breaking the cascade thread or blocking public crowd chats.
>
